<browser_instruction># Claude in Chrome browser automation

You have access to browser automation tools (mcp__claude-in-chrome__*) for interacting with web pages in Chrome. Follow these guidelines for effective browser automation.

## Loading deferred tools

If the mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools are deferred (must be loaded via ToolSearch before use), load every tool you expect to need in ONE ToolSearch call — the select query accepts a comma-separated list — never one call per tool. Start with the core set:

ToolSearch with query "select:mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__navigate,mcp__claude-in-chrome__computer,mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_page,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp,mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_close_mcp"

Add task-specific tools to the same call when the task obviously needs them: read_console_messages / read_network_requests for debugging, form_input for forms, gif_creator for recordings, javascript_tool for page scripting.

## GIF recording

When performing multi-step browser interactions that the user may want to review or share, use mcp__claude-in-chrome__gif_creator to record them.

You must ALWAYS:
* Capture extra frames before and after taking actions to ensure smooth playback
* Name the file meaningfully to help the user identify it later (e.g., "login_process.gif")

## Console log debugging

You can use mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages to read console output. Console output may be verbose. If you are looking for specific log entries, use the 'pattern' parameter with a regex-compatible pattern. This filters results efficiently and avoids overwhelming output. For example, use pattern: "[MyApp]" to filter for application-specific logs rather than reading all console output.

## Alerts and dialogs

IMPORTANT: Do not trigger JavaScript alerts, confirms, prompts, or browser modal dialogs through your actions. These browser dialogs block all further browser events and will prevent the extension from receiving any subsequent commands. Instead, when possible, use console.log for debugging and then use the mcp__claude-in-chrome__read_console_messages tool to read those log messages. If a page has dialog-triggering elements:
1. Avoid clicking buttons or links that may trigger alerts (e.g., "Delete" buttons with confirmation dialogs)
2. If you must interact with such elements, warn the user first that this may interrupt the session
3. Use mcp__claude-in-chrome__javascript_tool to check for and dismiss any existing dialogs before proceeding

If you accidentally trigger a dialog and lose responsiveness, inform the user they need to manually dismiss it in the browser.

## Avoid rabbit holes and loops

When using browser automation tools, stay focused on the specific task. If you encounter any of the following, stop and ask the user for guidance:
- Unexpected complexity or tangential browser exploration
- Browser tool calls failing or returning errors after 2-3 attempts
- No response from the browser extension
- Page elements not responding to clicks or input
- Pages not loading or timing out
- Unable to complete the browser task despite multiple approaches

Explain what you attempted, what went wrong, and ask how the user would like to proceed. Do not keep retrying the same failing browser action or explore unrelated pages without checking in first.

## Tab context and session startup

IMPORTANT: At the start of each browser automation session, call mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_context_mcp first to get information about the user's current browser tabs. Use this context to understand what the user might want to work with before creating new tabs.

Never reuse tab IDs from a previous/other session. Follow these guidelines:
1. Only reuse an existing tab if the user explicitly asks to work with it
2. Otherwise, create a new tab with mcp__claude-in-chrome__tabs_create_mcp
3. If a tool returns an error indicating the tab doesn't exist or is invalid, call tabs_context_mcp to get fresh tab IDs
4. When a tab is closed by the user or a navigation error occurs, call tabs_context_mcp to see what tabs are available</browser_instruction>

SC2002 Restaurant Rush: TA Marking Rubric

 SC2002 Restaurant Rush: TA Marking Rubric

  For TAs | Marking the final submission (no live demo)
  Use this rubric to mark the final submission: the Java project, report, stage tags, README
  and recorded video. There is no live demonstration.

 Key principles

      Credit evidence wherever it appears, in any submitted artefact.
      Do not deduct twice for the same gap, such as a missing video clip and the underlying
      feature.
      Real player decisions are required. The player must manage limited staff tasks.
      Automatic random orders alone do not meet the game requirement.
      Score each line item independently. If the evidence is absent, that line item gets 0.
      Feedback: give at least one concrete strength and one concrete improvement.

 1 Review procedure

   1. Check the submission. Confirm the repository and the stage-1 , stage-2 and
       stage-3 tags. Check the README build command and video timestamps.

   2. Run the game. Play or inspect stage-3 with the base settings, using the README's
      reproducible commands to try the victory and defeat paths. Run stage-1 if it is
      unclear whether it works on its own.

   3. Watch the video. Look for an actual manager decision, the food lifecycle, a result and
      the stage changes.

   4. Compare the stages on GitHub. Open stage-1...stage-2 and stage-2...stage-3 .
      Record the existing source files changed or deleted, read the group's explanation, and
      inspect the code for type checking or tightly coupled rules.

   5. Spot-check the diagrams. Compare the class and sequence diagrams against one run.
   6. Mark and give feedback. Score each line item (Section 3), then complete the mark

      sheet (Section 6). If the submission cannot be run, record the limitation.

 2 Awarding partial credit

  The marks listed for each line item are the maximum. Use these anchors:

                VIP loses 5 per waiting turn and gets 10% off the

## 0015 User — 2026-11-03T02:53:39.576Z

Source record: 61; UUID: 863057fb-f446-4e9b-9a16-b17f0b1fd6c1.

### Tool result: toolu_01JyEFyK56H8D22BcRH8kwZD

1	# SC2002 Restaurant Rush
2	
3	A turn-based restaurant management game for the command line, written in Java for the SC2002 group project.
4	The player is the manager: staff time is limited, so the manager decides which customer or order comes first.
5	
6	Repository: https://github.com/RobotHanzo/SC2002GroupProject
7	
8	## Build and run
9	
10	Needs JDK 17 or newer. No other dependencies, no network. Run every command from the repository root.
11	
12	```
13	javac --release 17 -d out -sourcepath src src/restaurantrush/Main.java
14	java -cp out restaurantrush.Main
15	```
16	
17	Replay a recorded run instead of typing the choices (the file is read and echoed, so the screen shows a transcript):
18	
19	```
20	java -cp out restaurantrush.Main --input runs/victory.txt
21	java -cp out restaurantrush.Main --input runs/defeat.txt
22	```
23	
24	Run the automated checks (see [docs/test-notes.md](docs/test-notes.md) for what they cover and for the expected results of both runs):
25	
26	```
27	javac --release 17 -d out -sourcepath src test/restaurantrush/GameTests.java
28	java -cp out restaurantrush.GameTests
29	```
30	
31	## Stage tags
32	
33	Each stage is a separate tagged commit that builds and runs on its own. `runs/` and the tests in each tag belong to that stage, so the recorded runs of an earlier stage are in its tag (`git checkout stage-1`).
34	
35	| Tag | Adds |
36	|---|---|
37	| `stage-1` | Basic operation: Regular customers, menu, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Table, Order, the waiting queue, the full six-step turn, abandonment, victory and defeat |
38	| `stage-2` | Customer behaviour: VIP and Critic customers next to the Regulars |
39	| `stage-3` | New role and menu policy: the Host, ComboMeal and Happy Hour |
40	
41	## How to play
42	
43	The base setting is fixed: 18 turns, 2 tables, one Waiter, one Chef, one Cashier, one Host (Stage 3), and one new customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16.
44	
45	| Menu entry | Price | Preparation units |
46	|---|---|---|
47	| Salad | $9.00 | 1 |
48	| Burger | $15.00 | 1 |
49	| Pasta | $18.00 | 2 |
50	| Salad + Burger (combo) | $21.60 | 2 |
51	| Salad + Pasta (combo) | $24.30 | 3 |
52	
53	A combo is made of two existing menu items: its price is 90% of the two prices added together (rounded to the nearest cent) and its preparation units are the sum. Customers pick their own food; the manager never does.
54	
55	| # | Customer | Arrives | Loses per waiting turn | Bill | Orders |
56	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
57	| 1 | VIP | turn 1 | 5 | 10% off | Burger |
58	| 2 | Regular | turn 4 | 8 | full price | Pasta |
59	| 3 | Regular | turn 7 | 8 | full price | Salad + Burger |
60	| 4 | Critic | turn 10 | 12 | full price | Salad + Pasta (the most expensive entry); loses a further 10 satisfaction if served more than 2 turns after the order was taken |
61	| 5 | Regular | turn 13 | 8 | full price | Salad |
62	| 6 | Regular | turn 16 | 8 | full price | Burger |
63	
64	Every turn the screen shows the turn number, revenue, the average satisfaction of paid customers, Happy Hour (and its remaining duration), the waiting queue, the tables, every customer's type, satisfaction and order status, and the tasks each staff member can do. Then you decide:
65	
66	| Prompt | Commands | What happens |
67	|---|---|---|
68	| Happy Hour (only while unused) | `y`, `n` (or Enter) | `y` switches Happy Hour on for this turn and the next; it can be used once per game |
69	| Host (one task per turn) | `SEAT <#>`, `WAIT` | seat a queued customer at a free table, in addition to the Waiter's task |
70	| Waiter (one task per turn) | `SEAT <#>`, `TAKE_ORDER <#>`, `SERVE <#>`, `WAIT` | seat a queued customer, take a seated customer's order, or serve a READY order |
71	| Chef (one unit per turn) | `COOK <#>`, `WAIT` | advance a customer's order by one preparation unit |
72	
73	`<#>` is the customer number shown on screen (`#2` and `2` both work). It may be left out when only one customer qualifies. A blank line means `WAIT`. A choice that cannot be done is explained and you choose again; it does not use up the staff task.
74	
75	After your choices the turn finishes on its own: customers who were served earlier finish eating (READY_TO_PAY), the Cashier charges at most one customer who became READY_TO_PAY on an earlier turn, and every customer who is still waiting for service loses satisfaction (the amount depends on the customer type). A customer who reaches 0 leaves without paying, the order is cancelled and the table is freed.
76	
77	After turn 18 you win if at least 4 customers have paid, revenue is at least $45.00 and the average satisfaction of the customers who paid is at least 60. Otherwise you lose. The game never ends early.
78	
79	### Happy Hour
80	
81	Switched on at the start of a turn, it lasts for that turn and the next, and the screen shows the turns left. While it is on:
82	
83	- orders taken get 20% off their bill. The VIP discount is applied first, then Happy Hour: a VIP's Burger is $15.00 x 0.9 x 0.8 = $10.80; a Regular's Pasta is $14.40;
84	- at the start of each active turn, customers still waiting for a table, to order, or for food recover 15 satisfaction (never above 100).
85	
86	The bill is locked when the order is taken, so Happy Hour never changes an order already placed and never touches customers who have left.
87	
88	### Rules this implementation fixes where the brief leaves a choice
89	
90	- **Food choice.** Regular and VIP customers take menu entry number `customerId mod 5` (the menu has five entries: Salad 0, Burger 1, Pasta 2, Salad + Burger 3, Salad + Pasta 4), so customer 1 orders a Burger, 2 a Pasta, 3 the Salad + Burger combo, 4 the Salad + Pasta combo, 5 a Salad. The Critic ignores that rule and takes the most expensive entry. The choice is shown when the order is taken.
91	- **Price lock.** The bill is worked out when the Waiter takes the order (customer's own pricing first, then Happy Hour) and is stored in the Order; the Cashier collects that amount.
92	- **Critic reaction.** "Served more than 2 turns after the order was taken" means the serving turn minus the order turn is 3 or more. A 3-unit combo ordered on turn *n* is READY at the end of turn *n*+2 at the earliest and served on turn *n*+3, so the Critic's combo always costs the extra 10 satisfaction; with a 2-unit dish a prompt Waiter avoids it.
93	- **Order of events in a turn.** Arrival, Happy Hour choice and recovery, your Host choice, your Waiter choice, your Chef choice (so the Chef can work on an order taken this turn, and the Waiter can take the order of a customer the Host has just seated), eating and payment, then waiting and abandonment. A dish the Chef finishes this turn is READY only after the Waiter has acted, so it is served next turn at the earliest.
94	- **Eating and payment.** A customer served on turn *n* is READY_TO_PAY on turn *n*+1 and can pay on turn *n*+2. "Oldest first" means the customer who arrived first.
95

All turn guards (`canFinishEating`, `canPay`) and the status rules are untouched |
117	| `entity/Order.java` | Constructor takes the bill and the turn it was taken; `getPriceCents()` became `getBillCents()` | The VIP discount means the price is no longer the item's list price, so the bill is locked in the order when it is taken. The status transitions are untouched |
118	| `entity/Waiter.java` | `takeOrder` takes a `Menu` and the turn, asks the customer for the dish and the bill, and returns the `Order`; `serveOrder` calls `customer.onServed` | This is where type-specific behaviour is dispatched: the Waiter calls the methods on `Customer` and never checks the type |

SC2002 Restaurant Rush Group Assignment
Java command-line turn-based restaurant management game  |  Student brief
Your group builds a playable restaurant management game in Java. The player is the manager. Staff time is limited, so the manager decides which customers and orders come first. You build the game in three stages, and each stage must show how your object-oriented design supports the next set of requirements.
Item
Summary
What you build
A turn-based restaurant game with a command-line interface, in three runnable stages.
Stages
All three stages are published now and submitted together. Each stage is a tagged, runnable version in GitHub.
Interface
A command-line interface is enough. A GUI is optional and earns no extra marks by itself.
Submission
One zip per group: source code, README, PDF report, recorded video and declarations (Section 6).
Assessment
No live demo. Marks come from your submitted files, GitHub repository and video (Section 7).
1  Learning outcomes
Assign clear responsibilities to objects and use encapsulation, inheritance, polymorphism and composition appropriately.
Explain how the design changed across three working stages using UML, code and GitHub evidence.
Implement a complete game with meaningful player choices, a clear result and robust input handling.
2  Game setup
2.1  Base setting
Your game must always offer this base setting, so that a TA can reproduce your runs. Other difficulty settings are optional.
Setting
Required base value
Turns
18 turns maximum
Tables
2
Staff
Waiter × 1 (one task per turn)
Chef × 1 (one preparation unit per turn)
Cashier × 1 (one payment per turn)
Arrivals
One customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16 (six in total)
Objective
At the end of turn 18: at least 4 paid customers AND revenue at least $45 AND average satisfaction of paid customers at least 60
Menu item

$9.00

$15.00

2.2  General rules
Customers choose their own food. The manager never chooses it. Use a documented deterministic rule or seeded randomness, and show the choice once the order is placed.
Keep rules out of the UI. The UI only collects the manager’s decisions and displays the state. Game rules belong in domain and control objects.
Money. If fractional prices arise, store amounts as integer cents. Display dollars with two decimals.
Offline. All mandatory features must work without network access.
3  How a turn works
3.1  Start-of-turn display
At the start of every turn, show: the turn number, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, the waiting queue, occupied tables, each customer’s satisfaction, order status, and the staff tasks available.
3.2  Turn steps
Each turn then runs these six steps in order.
Step
What happens
1  Arrivals
The scheduled customer joins the waiting queue with satisfaction 100.
2  Manager choices
Waiter: choose at most one task, or Wait: seat one queued customer at a free table, take one seated customer’s order, or serve one READY order.
Chef: choose at most one order to advance by one preparation unit, or Wait. The Chef may work on an order the Waiter took this turn.
3  Kitchen and serving
The Waiter acts before the Chef. When an order’s last preparation unit is done, it becomes READY. A dish can be served only if it was already READY when the Waiter acts, so a dish finished this turn is served next turn at the earliest. Serving sets it to SERVED.
4  Eating and payment
A customer served on an earlier turn finishes one full turn of eating and becomes READY_TO_PAY. The Cashier automatically handles at most one READY_TO_PAY customer per turn, oldest first. A customer who became READY_TO_PAY this turn pays next turn at the earliest. Payment adds the bill to revenue, marks the customer and order PAID, and frees the table.
5  Waiting and abandonment
At turn end, every waiting or seated customer who has not been served loses satisfaction (Regular: 8 points, minimum 0). A customer who reaches 0 leaves without paying: cancel any unfinished order and free the table. Customers who are served, eating or ready to pay lose nothing.
6  Result
The game always runs all 18 turns; there is no early ending. After turn 18: Victory if all three targets in Section 2.1 are met, otherwise Defeat.
3.3  Example: one customer from arrival to payment
This example follows one Regular customer who orders Pasta (2 preparation units) and gets the manager’s full attention.
Turn
What happens
Satisfaction at turn end
1
Arrives (100). Waiter seats the customer.
92 (seated, not served)
2
Waiter takes the Pasta order. Chef does unit 1 of 2.
84
3
Chef does unit 2 of 2: order is READY. The Waiter cannot serve it this turn.
76
4
Waiter serves: SERVED.
76 (no further loss)
5
Customer finishes eating: READY_TO_PAY.
76
6
Cashier collects $18.00: PAID, table freed. Satisfaction 76 is recorded.
—
3.4  Rules that always apply
A Waiter completes at most one task per turn.
A table stays occupied until its customer pays or leaves.
A customer cannot eat before being served. The Cashier cannot charge before READY_TO_PAY.
An invalid choice must explain why it failed and let the manager choose again, without using up the staff task.
Revenue never includes unpaid or abandoned orders.
The customer who arrives on turn 16 cannot finish paying within 18 turns, so at most five customers can pay. This is intended, not a bug.
3.5  End-of-turn results
Show the served count, unhappy departures, revenue, average satisfaction of paid customers, and turns used. The average uses the satisfaction recorded when each customer pays. If no one has paid yet, show N/A.
4  The three stages
Each stage is a separate runnable version tagged in GitHub, and each builds on the one before.
Stage
Adds
Git tag
1  Basic operation
Regular customers, menu, Waiter, Chef, Cashier, full turn sequence, both endings
stage-1
2  Customer behaviour
VIP and Critic customers
stage-2
3  New role and menu policy
Host, ComboMeal, Happy Hour
stage-3
4.1  Stage 1: Basic restaurant operation
Build a complete playable game containing:
regular customers and the three menu items;
Waiter, Chef, Cashier, Table, Order and the waiting queue;
the full turn sequence, including satisfaction, abandonment, payment, victory and defeat.
The player sets seating, ordering and serving priority through the Waiter, and kitchen priority through the Chef.
Stage 1 must: compile and run on its own at tag stage-1; contain no Stage 2 or Stage 3 features; include one reproducible victory run and one reproducible defeat run in your test notes.
4.2  Stage 2: Customer behaviour extension
Add VIPCustomer and CriticCustomer alongside regular customers.
Customer

—

5

—

12
Full price
A distinct order preference or reaction to service that you define and document
A reproducible base-setting run must include at least one VIP and one Critic. You may replace two of the regular arrivals; there must still be six arrivals.
All customer types follow the same order and service flow.
Different behaviour must be dispatched through the customer objects, not through a chain of type checks in the turn controller.
Stage 2 is independently playable and uses the same win and lose criteria.
4.3  Stage 3: New role and menu policy
Host. The Host seats at most one queued customer per turn, in addition to the Waiter’s task. The Waiter can still seat customers.
ComboMeal. A combo is made of two existing menu items.
Preparation units = the sum of its two components.
Price = 90% of the components’ combined price, rounded to the nearest cent. Example: Salad + Pasta = ($9 + $18) × 0.9 = $24.30, 3 units.
A customer chooses a combo according to a rule you document.
Happy Hour. A pricing policy the manager may switch on.
Can be activated once per game, at the start of a turn. It lasts for that turn and the next. Display its remaining duration.
Orders taken while it is active get 20% off their bill.
During each active turn, customers still waiting for a table or food recover 15 satisfaction at turn start (maximum 100).
Apply the VIP discount first, then Happy Hour. The price is locked when the order is taken. Example: a VIP orders a Burger during Happy Hour: $15 × 0.9 × 0.8 = $10.80.
It does not change orders already placed, and does not affect customers who have left.
Stage 3 is independently playable.
4.4  Design freedom and changes to earlier code
You choose class names and design patterns. Do not create interfaces just to satisfy a checklist.
If a later stage needs you to modify an earlier class, explain why. Small registration or setup changes are expected.
If you broadly rewrite stable game rules, explain why and show that earlier behaviour still works.
Extra features earn credit only when they show sound design and do not replace required rules.
5  Design evidence
Include these in your PDF report and repository.
Evidence
Requirements
Class diagram
Final design; must match the Stage 3 code.
Show the principal entity classes, necessary control and boundary classes, relevant enums, key attributes and operations with visibility, inheritance, and associations with multiplicities where relevant.
Annotate meaningful examples of encapsulation, polymorphism and composition.
Sequence diagram
One complete scenario in which a manager choice changes an outcome.
Include: customer arrival and order; Waiter and Chef assignments; READY then SERVED; eating; payment; the result check; one Stage 2 or 3 behaviour (e.g. VIP pricing or Happy Hour).
Show messages among the CLI, controller, staff and domain objects, with relevant guards and loops.
Design discussion
A table of key abstractions and their responsibilities.
SRP, OCP, LSP, ISP and DIP, only where genuinely reflected in your code.
Trade-offs, and one alternative design you considered.
How the player’s choices change a result.
GitHub evidence
Tags stage-1, stage-2 and stage-3 on runnable commits.
In the README, for each transition (1 → 2 and 2 → 3): new files; existing files changed or deleted, with reasons; the relevant commit or compare link.
The source code itself, not only the README, must show the real stages.
6  Submission
6.1  What to submit
Submit one archive per group through the course assignment link by Nov. 15, 2026 11:59pm. Name it LABGroup_AssignmentGrp.zip, for example FCS1_Grp1.zip.
Item
Contents
Source code
Java source with build and run instructions
README
Repository URL, stage comparisons (Section 5) and video timestamps
PDF report
Final diagrams, design discussion, functional evidence and reflections
Video
The recorded video, or an accessible download link if the file is too large
Declarations
Signed Declaration of Original Work, and each member’s Declaration on Use of GAI Assistance (course forms)
6.2  Recorded video
There is no live demonstration. Record one group video of about 15 minutes with readable screen capture and audible explanation. Each member explains a part they understand. List the timestamps of these segments in your README:
#
Segment
1
The game launching from the submitted project
2
Meaningful manager choices, including a decision where staff are constrained
3
A dish going READY → SERVED → payment
4
A VIP or Critic behaviour
5
A Stage 3 feature
6
One victory and one defeat (separate reproducible runs, or an accelerated replay)
7
The stage tag comparisons and the diagrams
The video is evidence for the marking criteria, not a separate score. If you submit a link, it must stay available to graders.

Criterion

21	/**
22	 * Runs the game: 18 turns of six steps each (arrivals, manager choices, kitchen and serving,
23	 * eating and payment, waiting and abandonment, result). It owns the turn counter and decides
24	 * whether a choice is legal; the CLI only collects choices and displays state.
25	 */
26	public class GameController {
27	    public static final int REQUIRED_PAID_CUSTOMERS = 4;
28	    public static final int REQUIRED_REVENUE_CENTS = 4500;
29	    public static final int REQUIRED_AVERAGE_SATISFACTION = 60;
30	
31	    private static final int MAX_TURNS = 18;
32	
33	    private final Restaurant restaurant;
34	    private final ArrivalSchedule schedule;
35	    private int currentTurn;
36	    private final int maxTurns;
37	    private final RestaurantCLI cli;
38	
39	    public GameController(RestaurantCLI cli) {
40	        this.cli = cli;
41	        this.restaurant = new Restaurant();
42	        this.schedule = new ArrivalSchedule();
43	        this.maxTurns = MAX_TURNS;
44	    }
45	
46	    public int getCurrentTurn() {
47	        return currentTurn;
48	    }
49	
50	    public int getMaxTurns() {
51	        return maxTurns;
52	    }
53	
54	    /** Plays all turns (there is no early ending), then reports victory or defeat. */
55	    public void startGame() {
56	        while (currentTurn < maxTurns) {
57	            currentTurn++;
58	            executeTurn();
59	        }
60	        cli.displayResult(evaluateGameResult(), restaurant);
61	    }
62	
63	    private void executeTurn() {
64	        processScheduledArrival();
65	        cli.displayTurnStatus(restaurant);
66	        processHappyHourActivation();
67	        applyHappyHourRecovery();
68	        coordinateHostAction();
69	        coordinateStaffActions();
70	        coordinatePaymentProcessing();
71	        processEndOfTurnUpdates();
72	    }
73	
74	    /** Step 1: the schedule decides who arrives at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16. */
75	    private void processScheduledArrival() {
76	        if (schedule.isArrivalTurn(currentTurn)) {
77	            restaurant.registerArrival(schedule.createArrival(currentTurn));
78	        }
79	    }
80	
81	    /** Start of turn: while Happy Hour is unused the manager may switch it on (once per game). */
82	    private void processHappyHourActivation() {
83	        HappyHour happyHour = restaurant.getHappyHour();
84	        if (happyHour.isAvailable() && cli.readHappyHourChoice() && happyHour.activate()) {
85	            cli.displayMessage("Happy Hour is ON: orders taken now get a discount and waiting customers recover "
86	                    + "satisfaction. Turns left: " + happyHour.getRemainingTurns() + ".");
87	        }
88	    }
89	
90	    /** Start of an active Happy Hour turn: customers still waiting for a table or food recover satisfaction. */
91	    private void applyHappyHourRecovery() {
92	        HappyHour happyHour = restaurant.getHappyHour();
93	        if (!happyHour.isActive()) {
94	            return;
95	        }
96	        int amount = happyHour.getRecoveryAmount();
97	        List<String> changes = new ArrayList<>();
98	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
99	            if (customer.isAwaitingService()) {
100	                int before = customer.getSatisfaction();
101	                customer.recoverSatisfaction(amount);
102	                changes.add("#" + customer.getId() + " " + before + " -> " + customer.getSatisfaction());
103	            }
104	        }
105	        cli.displayMessage("Happy Hour (" + happyHour.getRemainingTurns() + " turn(s) left including this one): "
106	                + (changes.isEmpty() ? "nobody is waiting." : "waiting customers recover " + String.join(", ", changes) + "."));
107	    }
108	
109	    /** Step 2 (Host): at most one queued customer is seated, on top of the Waiter's own task. */
110	    private void coordinateHostAction() {
111	        chooseUntilAccepted(cli::readHostChoice, this::performHostTask);
112	    }
113	
114	    /** Steps 2 and 3: the Waiter acts first, then the Chef. A rejected choice does not use up the task. */
115	    private void coordinateStaffActions() {
116	        chooseUntilAccepted(cli::readWaiterChoice, this::performWaiterTask);
117	        chooseUntilAccepted(cli::readChefChoice, this::performChefTask);
118	    }
119	
120	    private void chooseUntilAccepted(Supplier<String> reader, Consumer<String> task) {
121	        while (true) {
122	            String choice = reader.get();
123	            try {
124	                task.accept(choice);
125	                return;
126	            } catch (IllegalArgumentException | IllegalStateException rejected) {
127	                cli.displayError(rejected.getMessage());
128	            }
129	        }
130	    }
131	
132	    private void performWaiterTask(String choice) {
133	        Command command = Command.parse(choice);
134	        switch (command.verb) {
135	            case "WAIT" -> { }
136	            case "SEAT" -> seatQueuedCustomer(command, restaurant.getWaiter()::seatCustomer, "Waiter");
137	            case "TAKE_ORDER", "ORDER", "TAKE" -> takeOrder(command);
138	            case "SERVE" -> serve(command);
139	            default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException(
140	                    "Unknown Waiter command '" + command.verb + "'. Use SEAT, TAKE_ORDER, SERVE or WAIT.");
141	        }
142	    }
143	
144	    private void performHostTask(String choice) {
145	        Command command = Command.parse(choice);
146	        switch (command.verb) {
147	            case "WAIT" -> { }
148	            case "SEAT" -> seatQueuedCustomer(command, restaurant.getHost()::seatCustomer, "Host");
149	            default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException(
150	                    "Unknown Host command '" + command.verb + "'. Use SEAT or WAIT.");
151	        }
152	    }
153	
154	    private void performChefTask(String choice) {
155	        Command command = Command.parse(choice);
156	        switch (command.verb) {
157	            case "WAIT" -> { }
158	            case "COOK", "PREPARE", "ADVANCE" -> prepare(command);
159	            default -> throw new IllegalArgumentException(
160	                    "Unknown Chef command '" + command.verb + "'. Use COOK or WAIT.");
161	        }
162	    }
163	
164	    private void seatQueuedCustomer(Command command, BiConsumer<Customer, Table> seater, String actor) {
165	        Customer customer = select(command, new ArrayList<>(restaurant.getWaitingQueue()),
166	                "nobody is in the waiting queue", "only a customer in the waiting queue can be seated");
167	        Table table = restaurant.findAvailableTable();
168	        if (table == null) {
169	            throw new IllegalStateException("Both tables are occupied; a table frees up when its customer pays or leaves");
170	        }
171	        seater.accept(customer, table);
172	        restaurant.getWaitingQueue().remove(customer);
173	        cli.displayMessage(actor + " seats " + describe(customer) + " at Table " + table.getTableId() + ".");
174	    }
175	
176	    private void takeOrder(Command command) {
177	        Customer customer = select(command, customersWithStatus(CustomerStatus.SEATED),
178	                "no seated customer is waiting to order", "only a seated customer can order");
179	        Order order = restaurant.getWaiter().takeOrder(customer, restaurant.getMenu(), restaurant.getHappyHour(), currentTurn);
180	        MenuItem item = order.getItem();
181	        cli.displayMessage("Waiter takes the order of " + describe(customer) + ". They choose "
182	                + item.getName() + " (list price " + Money.format(item.getPriceCents()) + ", "
183	                + item.getPreparationUnits() + " preparation unit(s)). Bill locked at "
184	                + Money.format(order.getBillCents()) + ".");
185	    }
186	
187	    private void serve(Command command) {
188	        Customer customer = select(command, customersWithStatus(CustomerStatus.ORDERED),
189	                "no customer has an order to serve", "only a customer who has ordered can be served");
190	        restaurant.getWaiter().serveOrder(customer, currentTurn);
191	        cli.displayMessage("Waiter serves the " + customer.getOrder().getItem().getName() + " to "
192	                + describe(customer) + ".");
193	    }
194	
195	    private void prepare(Command command) {
196	        Customer customer = select(command, customersWithStatus(CustomerStatus.ORDERED),
197	                "no order has been taken yet", "only a customer who has ordered has food to prepare");
198	        Order order = customer.getOrder();
199	        restaurant.getChef().prepare(order);
200	        cli.displayMessage("Chef works on the " + order.getItem().getName() + " for " + describe(customer)
201	                + ": " + order.getCompletedUnits() + "/" + order.getItem().getPreparationUnits()
202	                + " units, order is " + order.getStatus() + ".");
203	    }
204	
205	    /** Step 4: served customers finish eating, then the Cashier charges at most one READY_TO_PAY customer. */
206	    private void coordinatePaymentProcessing() {
207	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
208	            if (customer.canFinishEating(currentTurn)) {
209	                customer.markReadyToPay(currentTurn);
210	                cli.displayMessage(describe(customer) + " finishes eating and is ready to pay.");
211	            }
212	        }
213	        Customer payer = restaurant.findOldestEligiblePayer(currentTurn);
214	        if (payer != null) {
215	            int bill = payer.getOrder().getBillCents();
216	            restaurant.getCashier().processPayment(payer, restaurant);
217	            cli.displayMessage("Cashier collects " + Money.format(bill) + " from " + describe(payer)
218	                    + " (satisfaction " + payer.getSatisfaction() + "). The table is free again.");
219	        }
220	    }
221	
222	    /** Step 5: customers who are still waiting lose satisfaction; at 0 they leave without paying. */
223	    private void processEndOfTurnUpdates() {
224	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
225	            if (!customer.isAwaitingService()) {
226	                continue;
227	            }
228	            customer.reduceSatisfaction();
229	            if (customer.shouldAbandon()) {
230	                restaurant.handleAbandonment(customer);
231	                cli.displayMessage(describe(customer)
232	                        + " runs out of patience and leaves without paying. Any unfinished order is cancelled.");
233	            }
234	        }
235	        restaurant.getHappyHour().endTurn();
236	    }
237	
238	    /** Step 6: victory needs all three targets. */
239	    private boolean evaluateGameResult() {
240	        return restaurant.getPaidCustomerCount() >= REQUIRED_PAID_CUSTOMERS
241	                && restaurant.getRevenueCents() >= REQUIRED_REVENUE_CENTS
242	                && restaurant.getAverageSatisfaction() >= REQUIRED_AVERAGE_SATISFACTION;
243	    }
244	
245	    /** Resolves the customer a command refers to, or explains why it cannot. */
246	    private Customer select(Command command, List<Customer> candidates, String noCandidates, String ineligible) {
247	        if (command.customerId == null) {
248	            if (candidates.isEmpty()) {
249	                throw new IllegalStateException("Nothing to do: " + noCandidates);
250	            }
251	            if (candidates.size() > 1) {
252	                throw new IllegalArgumentException("Several customers qualify (" + idList(candidates)
253	                        + "); add a customer number, for example " + command.verb + " " + candidates.get(0).getId());
254	            }
255	            return candidates.get(0);
256	        }
257	        for (Customer candidate : candidates) {
258	            if (candidate.getId() == command.customerId) {
259	                return candidate;
260	            }
261	        }
262	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
263	            if (customer.getId() == command.customerId) {
264	                throw new IllegalStateException(describe(customer) + " is " + customer.getStatus()
265	                        + ": " + ineligible);
266	            }
267	        }
268	        throw new IllegalArgumentException("There is no customer #" + command.customerId);
269	    }
270	
271	    private List<Customer> customersWithStatus(CustomerStatus status) {
272	        List<Customer> matching = new ArrayList<>();
273	        for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
274	            if (customer.getStatus() == status) {
275	                matching.add(customer);
276	            }
277	        }
278	        return matching;
279	    }
280	
281	    private static String describe(Customer customer) {
282	        return "Customer #" + customer.getId() + " (" + customer.getTypeLabel() + ")";
283	    }
284	
285	    private static String idList(Collection<Customer> customers) {
286	        StringBuilder ids = new StringBuilder();
287	        for (Customer customer : customers) {
288	            ids.append(ids.length() == 0 ? "#" : ", #").append(customer.getId());
289	        }
290	        return ids.toString();
291	    }
292	
293	    /** A manager command: a verb and an optional customer number. A blank line means WAIT. */
294	    private static final class Command {
295	        private final String verb;
296	        private final Integer customerId;
297	
298	        private Command(String verb, Integer customerId) {
299	            this.verb = verb;
300	            this.customerId = customerId;
301	        }
302	
303	        private static Command parse(String raw) {
304	            String text = raw == null ? "" : raw.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
305	            if (text.isEmpty()) {
306	                return new Command("WAIT", null);
307	            }
308	            String[] words = text.split("\\s+");
309	            if (words.length > 2) {
310	                throw new IllegalArgumentException("Expected 'COMMAND [customer number]' but got '" + raw.trim() + "'");
311	            }
312	            Integer id = null;
313	            if (words.length == 2) {
314	                String number = words[1].startsWith("#") ? words[1].substring(1) : words[1];
315	                try {
316	                    id = Integer.valueOf(number);
317	                } catch (NumberFormatException notANumber) {
318	                    throw new IllegalArgumentException("'" + words[1] + "' is not a customer number");
319	                }
320	            }
321	            return new Command(words[0], id);
322	        }
323	    }
324	}

### Tool result: toolu_013jte77R1WWpPEa3oN8kzMC

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	/**
4	 * The Waiter seats customers, takes their orders and serves finished dishes. The controller lets
5	 * the Waiter do at most one of these per turn; each method rejects an impossible request by
6	 * throwing, before anything is changed.
7	 */
8	public class Waiter {
9	
10	    public void seatCustomer(Customer customer, Table table) {
11	        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.WAITING) {
12	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
13	                    + "; only a customer in the waiting queue can be seated");
14	        }
15	        if (!table.isAvailable()) {
16	            throw new IllegalStateException("Table " + table.getTableId() + " is already occupied");
17	        }
18	        table.seat(customer);
19	        customer.markSeated();
20	    }
21	
22	    /**
23	     * The customer chooses the dish and works out their own bill (a VIP gets a discount); then the
24	     * Happy Hour discount, if active, is applied on top. The bill is locked in the new Order.
25	     */
26	    public Order takeOrder(Customer customer, Menu menu, HappyHour happyHour, int currentTurn) {
27	        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.SEATED) {
28	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
29	                    + "; only a seated customer can order");
30	        }
31	        MenuItem item = customer.chooseMenuItem(menu);
32	        int billCents = happyHour.applyDiscount(customer.calculateBillCents(item.getPriceCents()));
33	        Order order = new Order(item, billCents, currentTurn);
34	        customer.assignOrder(order);
35	        return order;
36	    }
37	
38	    public void serveOrder(Customer customer, int currentTurn) {
39	        Order order = customer.getOrder();
40	        if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.ORDERED || order == null) {
41	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
42	                    + "; there is no order waiting to be served");
43	        }
44	        if (!order.isReady()) {
45	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + "'s " + order.getItem().getName()
46	                    + " is not READY yet (" + order.getStatus() + ", " + order.getCompletedUnits() + "/"
47	                    + order.getItem().getPreparationUnits() + " units)");
48	        }
49	        order.markServed();
50	        customer.markServed(currentTurn);
51	        customer.onServed(currentTurn - order.getTurnTaken());
52	    }
53	}
54

1	package restaurantrush.entity;
2	
3	/**
4	 * A restaurant guest. Every kind of customer follows the same order and service flow; subclasses
5	 * differ only in the hooks below (loss per waiting turn, billing, food choice, reaction to being
6	 * served), so the controller and staff call these on Customer and never check the type.
7	 * Satisfaction and status are private and change only through the methods of this class.
8	 * Lifecycle: WAITING -> SEATED -> ORDERED -> SERVED -> READY_TO_PAY -> PAID, or ABANDONED.
9	 */
10	public abstract class Customer {
11	    private static final int MAX_SATISFACTION = 100;
12	
13	    private final int customerId;
14	    private int satisfaction = MAX_SATISFACTION;
15	    private CustomerStatus status = CustomerStatus.WAITING;
16	    private Order order;
17	    private int servedTurn;
18	    private int readyTurn;
19	
20	    protected Customer(int customerId) {
21	        this.customerId = customerId;
22	    }
23	
24	    public int getId() {
25	        return customerId;
26	    }
27	
28	    public CustomerStatus getStatus() {
29	        return status;
30	    }
31	
32	    public int getSatisfaction() {
33	        return satisfaction;
34	    }
35	
36	    /** Short name of the customer type, for display. */
37	    public abstract String getTypeLabel();
38	
39	    /** Satisfaction lost at the end of each turn spent waiting for service. */
40	    protected abstract int getSatisfactionLoss();
41	
42	    /** Documented food rule: menu entry number (customerId mod menu size). The Critic overrides it. */
43	    public MenuItem chooseMenuItem(Menu menu) {
44	        return menu.getItem(customerId % menu.size());
45	    }
46	
47	    /** The bill for a dish at the given list price. The VIP overrides it to apply a discount. */
48	    public int calculateBillCents(int basePriceCents) {
49	        return basePriceCents;
50	    }
51	
52	    /** Called when the Waiter serves this customer; turnsWaited counts turns since the order was taken. */
53	    public void onServed(int turnsWaited) {
54	    }
55	
56	    /** Only subclasses may move satisfaction directly; the value always stays within 0..100. */
57	    protected void adjustSatisfaction(int delta) {
58	        satisfaction = Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_SATISFACTION, satisfaction + delta));
59	    }
60	
61	    /** End-of-turn loss for a customer who has not been served yet; everyone else loses nothing. */
62	    public void reduceSatisfaction() {
63	        if (isAwaitingService()) {
64	            adjustSatisfaction(-getSatisfactionLoss());
65	        }
66	    }
67	
68	    /** Happy Hour: a customer who is still waiting for a table or food regains satisfaction (never above 100). */
69	    public void recoverSatisfaction(int amount) {
70	        if (isAwaitingService()) {
71	            adjustSatisfaction(amount);
72	        }
73	    }
74	
75	    public void assignOrder(Order newOrder) {
76	        require(CustomerStatus.SEATED);
77	        order = newOrder;
78	        status = CustomerStatus.ORDERED;
79	    }
80	
81	    public Order getOrder() {
82	        return order;
83	    }
84	
85	    public void markSeated() {
86	        require(CustomerStatus.WAITING);
87	        status = CustomerStatus.SEATED;
88	    }
89	
90	    public void markServed(int turn) {
91	        require(CustomerStatus.ORDERED);
92	        servedTurn = turn;
93	        status = CustomerStatus.SERVED;
94	    }
95	
96	    /** A served customer eats for one full turn: allowed from the turn after the serving turn. */
97	    public boolean canFinishEating(int turn) {
98	        return status == CustomerStatus.SERVED && turn > servedTurn;
99	    }
100	
101	    public void markReadyToPay(int turn) {
102	        if (!canFinishEating(turn)) {
103	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customerId + " has not finished eating yet");
104	        }
105	        readyTurn = turn;
106	        status = CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY;
107	    }
108	
109	    /** The Cashier may charge from the turn after the customer became READY_TO_PAY. */
110	    public boolean canPay(int turn) {
111	        return status == CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY && turn > readyTurn;
112	    }
113	
114	    public void markPaid() {
115	        require(CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY);
116	        status = CustomerStatus.PAID;
117	    }
118	
119	    public boolean shouldAbandon() {
120	        return isAwaitingService() && satisfaction <= 0;
121	    }
122	
123	    public void markAbandoned() {
124	        if (!isAwaitingService()) {
125	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customerId + " is " + status + " and cannot abandon");
126	        }
127	        status = CustomerStatus.ABANDONED;
128	    }
129	
130	    /** True while the customer is waiting for a table, to order, or for food (not yet served). */
131	    public boolean isAwaitingService() {
132	        return status == CustomerStatus.WAITING || status == CustomerStatus.SEATED
133	                || status == CustomerStatus.ORDERED;
134	    }
135	
136	    private void require(CustomerStatus expected) {
137	        if (status != expected) {
138	            throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customerId + " is " + status + ", not " + expected);
139	        }
140	    }
141	}
142

 40 │     public void registerArrival(Customer customer) {

 43 │     }

 50 │             }
 51 │         }

 53 │     }

 56 │     public Customer findOldestEligiblePayer(int turn) {
 57 │         for (Customer customer : customers) {

102 │     public void recordPayment(int amountCents) {
103 │         if (amountCents < 0) {
104 │             throw new IllegalArgumentException("A payment cannot be negative");
105 │         }
106 │         revenueCents += amountCents;
107 │     }

109 │     public void releaseTableOf(Customer customer) {
110 │         for (Table table : tables) {
111 │             if (table.getOccupied() == customer) {

118 │         return countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.PAID);
119 │     }
120 │ 
121 │     public int getServedCustomerCount() {
122 │         return countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.SERVED) + countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY)
123 │                 + countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.PAID);
124 │     }
125 │ 
126 │     /** Mean satisfaction of paid customers, as recorded when each paid; 0 when nobody has paid. */
127 │     public double getAverageSatisfaction() {
128 │         int total = 0;
129 │         int paid = 0;
130 │         for (Customer customer : customers) {
131 │             if (customer.getStatus() == CustomerStatus.PAID) {
132 │                 total += customer.getSatisfaction();
133 │                 paid++;
134 │             }
135 │         }
136 │         return paid == 0 ? 0.0 : (double) total / paid;
137 │     }
138 │

140 │         return revenueCents;
141 │     }
142 │ 
143 │     /** A customer whose satisfaction hit 0 leaves: cancel the unfinished order, free the table, leave the queue. */
144 │     public void handleAbandonment(Customer customer) {
145 │         customer.markAbandoned();
146 │         Order order = customer.getOrder();
147 │         if (order != null) {
148 │             order.cancel();
149 │         }
150 │         releaseTableOf(customer);
151 │         waitingQueue.remove(customer);
152 │     }
153 │ 
154 │     public int getUnhappyDepartureCount() {
155 │         return countWithStatus(CustomerStatus.ABANDONED);
156 │     }
157 │ 
158 │     private int countWithStatus(CustomerStatus status) {
159 │         int count = 0;
160 │         for (Customer customer : customers) {
161 │             if (customer.getStatus() == status) {
162 │                 count++;
163 │             }
164 │         }
165 │         return count;
166 │     }

######## entity/Order.java
 1 │ package restaurantrush.entity;
 2 │ 
 3 │ /**
 4 │  * One customer's order. The status is private and moves only through the methods below, so an
 5 │  * illegal jump (for example serving a dish that is not READY) is rejected here, not in the UI.
 6 │  * Legal: PLACED -> PREPARING -> READY -> SERVED -> PAID, with PLACED -> READY for a 1-unit dish,
 7 │  * and PLACED / PREPARING / READY -> CANCELLED when the customer leaves.
 8 │  */
 9 │ public class Order {
10 │     private static int nextOrderId = 1;
11 │ 
12 │     private final int orderId;
13 │     private final MenuItem item;
14 │     private int completedUnits;
15 │     private OrderStatus status;
16 │     private final int billCents;
17 │     private final int turnTaken;
18 │ 
19 │     /** The bill is fixed here and has no setter: the price is locked when the order is taken. */
20 │     public Order(MenuItem item, int billCents, int turnTaken) {
21 │         this.orderId = nextOrderId++;
22 │         this.item = item;
23 │         this.billCents = billCents;
24 │         this.turnTaken = turnTaken;
25 │         this.status = OrderStatus.PLACED;
26 │     }
27 │ 
28 │     /** The Chef spends one preparation unit; the last unit makes the order READY. */
29 │     public void advancePreparation() {
30 │         if (status != OrderStatus.PLACED && status != OrderStatus.PREPARING) {
31 │             throw new IllegalStateException("The " + item.getName() + " order is " + status
32 │                     + " and cannot be prepared any further");
33 │         }
34 │         completedUnits++;
35 │         status = completedUnits >= item.getPreparationUnits() ? OrderStatus.READY : OrderStatus.PREPARING;
36 │     }
37 │ 
38 │     public boolean isReady() {
39 │         return status == OrderStatus.READY;
40 │     }
41 │ 
42 │     public void markServed() {
43 │         require(OrderStatus.READY, "served");
44 │         status = OrderStatus.SERVED;
45 │     }
46 │ 
47 │     public void markPaid() {
48 │         require(OrderStatus.SERVED, "paid");
49 │         status = OrderStatus.PAID;
50 │     }
51 │ 
52 │     public void cancel() {
53 │         if (status != OrderStatus.PLACED && status != OrderStatus.PREPARING && status != OrderStatus.READY) {
54 │             throw new IllegalStateException("A " + status + " order cannot be cancelled");
55 │         }
56 │         status = OrderStatus.CANCELLED;
57 │     }
58 │ 
59 │     public OrderStatus getStatus() {
60 │         return status;
61 │     }
62 │ 
63 │     public int getOrderId() {
64 │         return orderId;
65 │     }
66 │ 
67 │     public MenuItem getItem() {
68 │         return item;
69 │     }
70 │ 
71 │     public int getCompletedUnits() {
72 │         return completedUnits;
73 │     }
74 │ 
75 │     public int getBillCents() {
76 │         return billCents;
77 │     }
78 │ 
79 │     public int getTurnTaken() {
80 │         return turnTaken;
81 │     }
82 │ 
83 │     private void require(OrderStatus expected, String action) {
84 │         if (status != expected) {
85 │             throw new IllegalStateException("The " + item.getName() + " order is " + status
86 │                     + "; only a " + expected + " order can be " + action);
87 │         }
88 │     }
89 │ }
######## entity/Host.java
 1 │ package restaurantrush.entity;
 2 │ 
 3 │ /**
 4 │  * The Host seats at most one queued customer per turn, in addition to whatever the Waiter does
 5 │  * (the Waiter can still seat customers too). The controller allows one Host task per turn.
 6 │  */
 7 │ public class Host {
 8 │ 
 9 │     /** Rejects an occupied table or a customer who is not in the waiting queue, before changing anything. */
10 │     public void seatCustomer(Customer customer, Table table) {
11 │         if (!table.isAvailable()) {
12 │             throw new IllegalStateException("Table " + table.getTableId() + " is already occupied");
13 │         }
14 │         customer.markSeated();
15 │         table.seat(customer);
16 │     }
17 │ }
######## entity/HappyHour.java
 1 │ package restaurantrush.entity;
 2 │ 
 3 │ /**
 4 │  * A pricing policy the manager may switch on once per game, at the start of a turn. It lasts for
 5 │  * that turn and the next. While active, orders taken get 20% off and customers still waiting for
 6 │  * a table or food recover 15 satisfaction at the start of the turn. It never changes an order
 7 │  * that is already placed, because the bill is locked in the Order.
 8 │  */
 9 │ public class HappyHour {
10 │     private static final int DISCOUNT_PERCENT = 20;
11 │     private static final int RECOVERY = 15;
12 │     private static final int DURATION = 2;
13 │ 
14 │     private boolean used;
15 │     private int remainingTurns;
16 │ 
17 │     /** Switches Happy Hour on. Returns false (and changes nothing) if it was already used this game. */
18 │     public boolean activate() {
19 │         if (used) {
20 │             return false;
21 │         }
22 │         used = true;
23 │         remainingTurns = DURATION;
24 │         return true;
25 │     }
26 │ 
27 │     public boolean isActive() {
28 │         return remainingTurns > 0;
29 │     }
30 │ 
31 │     /** True until the manager switches Happy Hour on; it cannot be used a second time. */
32 │     public boolean isAvailable() {
33 │         return !used;
34 │     }
35 │ 
36 │     public int getRemainingTurns() {
37 │         return remainingTurns;
38 │     }
39 │ 
40 │     /** The bill after the Happy Hour discount (rounded to the nearest cent), or unchanged when it is not active. */
41 │     public int applyDiscount(int cents) {
42 │         if (!isActive()) {
43 │             return cents;
44 │         }
45 │         return (cents * (100 - DISCOUNT_PERCENT) + 50) / 100;
46 │     }
47 │ 
48 │     /** Satisfaction a waiting customer recovers at the start of an active turn; 0 when not active. */
49 │     public int getRecoveryAmount() {
50 │         return isActive() ? RECOVERY : 0;
51 │     }
52 │ 
53 │     /** Called once at the end of every turn. */
54 │     public void endTurn() {
55 │         if (remainingTurns > 0) {
56 │             remainingTurns--;
57 │         }
58 │     }
59 │ }

 5 │ /**
 6 │  * A combo is a menu item made of two existing menu items. It costs 90% of their combined price
 7 │  * (rounded to the nearest cent) and takes the sum of their preparation units, so the Waiter, Chef
 8 │  * and Cashier treat it like any other dish.

11 │     private static final int PRICE_PERCENT = 90;
12 │ 
13 │     private final MenuItem first;
14 │     private final MenuItem second;
15 │ 
16 │     public ComboMeal(MenuItem first, MenuItem second) {
17 │         super(first.getName() + " + " + second.getName(),
18 │                 ((first.getPriceCents() + second.getPriceCents()) * PRICE_PERCENT + 50) / 100,
19 │                 first.getPreparationUnits() + second.getPreparationUnits());
20 │         this.first = first;
21 │         this.second = second;
22 │     }
23 │ 
24 │     public List<MenuItem> getComponents() {
25 │         return List.of(first, second);
26 │     }
27 │ }

32 │         MenuItem best = items.get(0);
33 │         for (MenuItem item : items) {
34 │             if (item.getPriceCents() > best.getPriceCents()) {
35 │                 best = item;
36 │             }
37 │         }
38 │         return best;
39 │     }

######## entity/Cashier.java
 1 │ package restaurantrush.entity;
 2 │ 
 3 │ /** The Cashier collects the bill of one READY_TO_PAY customer, records the revenue and frees the table. */
 4 │ public class Cashier {
 5 │ 
 6 │     public void processPayment(Customer customer, Restaurant restaurant) {
 7 │         Order order = customer.getOrder();
 8 │         if (customer.getStatus() != CustomerStatus.READY_TO_PAY || order == null) {
 9 │             throw new IllegalStateException("Customer #" + customer.getId() + " is " + customer.getStatus()
10 │                     + "; the Cashier can only charge a READY_TO_PAY customer");
11 │         }
12 │         restaurant.recordPayment(order.getBillCents());
13 │         customer.markPaid();
14 │         order.markPaid();
15 │         restaurant.releaseTableOf(customer);
16 │     }
17 │ }

######## control/ArrivalSchedule.java
 1 │ package restaurantrush.control;
 2 │ 
 3 │ import java.util.List;
 4 │ import java.util.function.IntFunction;
 5 │ 
 6 │ import restaurantrush.entity.CriticCustomer;
 7 │ import restaurantrush.entity.Customer;
 8 │ import restaurantrush.entity.RegularCustomer;
 9 │ import restaurantrush.entity.VIPCustomer;
10 │ 
11 │ /**
12 │  * The base-setting arrivals: one customer at the start of turns 1, 4, 7, 10, 13 and 16. Customers
13 │  * are numbered in arrival order. Two of the six are special: the turn-1 arrival is a VIP and the
14 │  * turn-10 arrival is a Critic; the other four are Regulars.
15 │  */
16 │ public class ArrivalSchedule {
17 │     private final int[] arrivalTurns = {1, 4, 7, 10, 13, 16};
18 │     private final List<IntFunction<Customer>> customerTypes = List.of(
19 │             VIPCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new,
20 │             CriticCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new, RegularCustomer::new);
21 │     private int nextCustomerId = 1;
22 │ 
23 │     public boolean isArrivalTurn(int turn) {
24 │         return indexOf(turn) >= 0;
25 │     }
26 │ 
27 │     /** Creates the customer who arrives at the start of this turn. */
28 │     public Customer createArrival(int turn) {
29 │         int index = indexOf(turn);
30 │         if (index < 0) {
31 │             throw new IllegalArgumentException("Nobody arrives at the start of turn " + turn);
32 │         }
33 │         return customerTypes.get(index).apply(nextCustomerId++);
34 │     }
35 │ 
36 │     private int indexOf(int turn) {
37 │         for (int i = 0; i < arrivalTurns.length; i++) {
38 │             if (arrivalTurns[i] == turn) {
39 │                 return i;
40 │             }
41 │         }
42 │         return -1;
43 │     }
44 │ }
######## Main.java
 1 │ package restaurantrush;
 2 │ 
 3 │ import java.io.FileInputStream;
 4 │ import java.io.IOException;
 5 │ import java.io.InputStream;
 6 │ 
 7 │ import restaurantrush.boundary.RestaurantCLI;
 8 │ 
 9 │ /**
10 │  * Entry point. With no arguments the manager types the choices. With "--input choices.txt" the
11 │  * choices are read from a file instead (and echoed), which replays a recorded run.
12 │  */
13 │ public final class Main {
14 │     private Main() {
15 │     }
16 │ 
17 │     public static void main(String[] args) {
18 │         if (args.length == 0) {
19 │             new RestaurantCLI(System.in, System.out, false).start();
20 │         } else if (args.length == 2 && args[0].equals("--input")) {
21 │             try (InputStream choices = new FileInputStream(args[1])) {
22 │                 new RestaurantCLI(choices, System.out, true).start();
23 │             } catch (IOException unreadable) {
24 │                 System.err.println("Cannot read the choices file '" + args[1] + "': " + unreadable.getMessage());
25 │                 System.exit(1);
26 │             }
27 │         } else {
28 │             System.err.println("Usage: java -cp out restaurantrush.Main [--input choices.txt]");
29 │             System.exit(2);
30 │         }
31 │     }
32 │ }

 20 │ /**
 21 │  * The command-line boundary. It prints the game state and reads the manager's choices as text;
 22 │  * it contains no game rules (the controller decides whether a choice is legal).
 23 │  */
 24 │ public class RestaurantCLI {
 25 │     private final Scanner input;
 26 │     private final PrintStream out;
 27 │     private final boolean echoInput;
 28 │     private GameController controller;
 29 │ 
 30 │     /** echoInput repeats each line read, so a run fed from a file (stdin) reads like a transcript. */
 31 │     public RestaurantCLI(InputStream in, PrintStream out, boolean echoInput) {
 32 │         this.input = new Scanner(in);
 33 │         this.out = out;
 34 │         this.echoInput = echoInput;
 35 │     }
 36 │ 
 37 │     /** Runs one game. If the input ends before the game does, the run stops with a message. */
 38 │     public void start() {
 39 │         controller = new GameController(this);
 40 │         out.println("SC2002 Restaurant Rush - Stage 3");
 41 │         out.println("You manage a restaurant for " + controller.getMaxTurns() + " turns. Each turn the Waiter does one");
 42 │         out.println("task (SEAT, TAKE_ORDER or SERVE), the Host can also SEAT one queued customer, and the Chef");

 67 │     /** Asks whether to switch Happy Hour on now. Blank means no. */
 68 │     public boolean readHappyHourChoice() {
 69 │         while (true) {
 70 │             String answer = readLine("Happy Hour [y = switch on for this turn and the next | n or Enter = not now]: ")
 71 │                     .trim().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);
 72 │             if (answer.isEmpty() || answer.equals("n") || answer.equals("no")) {
 73 │                 return false;
 74 │             }
 75 │             if (answer.equals("y") || answer.equals("yes")) {
 76 │                 return true;
 77 │             }
 78 │             displayError("'" + answer + "' is not y or n");
 79 │         }
 80 │     }

131 │     private String readLine(String prompt) {
132 │         out.print(prompt);
133 │         out.flush();

141 │     private static String averageText(Restaurant restaurant) {
142 │         if (restaurant.getPaidCustomerCount() == 0) {
143 │             return "N/A";
144 │         }
145 │         return String.format(Locale.ROOT, "%.1f", restaurant.getAverageSatisfaction());
146 │     }
147 │ 
148 │     private static String happyHourText(HappyHour happyHour) {
149 │         if (happyHour.isActive()) {
150 │             return "ACTIVE, " + happyHour.getRemainingTurns() + " turn(s) left including this one";
151 │         }
152 │         return happyHour.isAvailable() ? "available (switch on once per game)" : "used";
153 │     }
154 │ 
155 │     private static String queueText(Restaurant restaurant) {
156 │         if (restaurant.getWaitingQueue().isEmpty()) {
157 │             return "empty";
158 │         }
159 │         List<String> entries = new ArrayList<>();
160 │         for (Customer customer : restaurant.getWaitingQueue()) {
161 │             entries.add("#" + customer.getId() + " " + customer.getTypeLabel() + " (satisfaction " + customer.getSatisfaction() + ")");
162 │         }
163 │         return String.join(" -> ", entries);
164 │     }
165 │ 
166 │     private static String tablesText(Restaurant restaurant) {
167 │         List<String> entries = new ArrayList<>();
168 │         for (Table table : restaurant.getTables()) {
169 │             Customer occupant = table.getOccupied();
170 │             entries.add("Table " + table.getTableId() + ": "
171 │                     + (occupant == null ? "free"
172 │                             : "#" + occupant.getId() + " " + occupant.getTypeLabel() + " (" + occupant.getStatus() + ")"));
173 │         }
174 │         return String.join(" | ", entries);
175 │     }
176 │ 
177 │     private static String orderText(Customer customer) {
178 │         Order order = customer.getOrder();
179 │         if (order == null) {
180 │             return "-";
181 │         }
182 │         return order.getItem().getName() + " " + order.getCompletedUnits() + "/"
183 │                 + order.getItem().getPreparationUnits() + " units, " + order.getStatus()
184 │                 + ", " + Money.format(order.getBillCents());
185 │     }
186 │ 
187 │     private static String hostTasksText(Restaurant restaurant) {
188 │         if (restaurant.getWaitingQueue().isEmpty() || restaurant.findAvailableTable() == null) {
189 │             return "none available (WAIT)";
190 │         }
191 │         return "SEAT " + ids(new ArrayList<>(restaurant.getWaitingQueue()));
192 │     }
193 │ 
194 │     private static String waiterTasksText(Restaurant restaurant) {
195 │         List<String> tasks = new ArrayList<>();
196 │         if (!restaurant.getWaitingQueue().isEmpty() && restaurant.findAvailableTable() != null) {
197 │             tasks.add("SEAT " + ids(new ArrayList<>(restaurant.getWaitingQueue())));
198 │         }
199 │         List<Customer> seated = withStatus(restaurant, CustomerStatus.SEATED, false);
200 │         if (!seated.isEmpty()) {
201 │             tasks.add("TAKE_ORDER " + ids(seated));
202 │         }
203 │         List<Customer> ready = withStatus(restaurant, CustomerStatus.ORDERED, true);
204 │         if (!ready.isEmpty()) {
205 │             tasks.add("SERVE " + ids(ready));
206 │         }
207 │         return tasks.isEmpty() ? "none available (WAIT)" : String.join("; ", tasks);
208 │     }
209 │ 
210 │     private static String chefTasksText(Restaurant restaurant) {
211 │         List<Customer> cooking = new ArrayList<>();
212 │         for (Customer customer : withStatus(restaurant, CustomerStatus.ORDERED, false)) {
213 │             if (!customer.getOrder().isReady()) {
214 │                 cooking.add(customer);
215 │             }
216 │         }
217 │         return cooking.isEmpty() ? "none available (WAIT)" : "COOK " + ids(cooking);
218 │     }
219 │ 
220 │     /** Customers with the given status; for ORDERED, readyOnly keeps those whose dish is READY. */
221 │     private static List<Customer> withStatus(Restaurant restaurant, CustomerStatus status, boolean readyOnly) {
222 │         List<Customer> matching = new ArrayList<>();
223 │         for (Customer customer : restaurant.getCustomers()) {
224 │             if (customer.getStatus() == status && (!readyOnly || customer.getOrder().isReady())) {
225 │                 matching.add(customer);
226 │             }
227 │         }
228 │         return matching;
229 │     }
230 │ 
231 │     private static String ids(List<Customer> customers) {
232 │         List<String> numbers = new ArrayList<>();
233 │         for (Customer customer : customers) {
234 │             numbers.add("#" + customer.getId());
235 │         }
236 │         return String.join(" ", numbers);
237 │     }
238 │ }

# Test notes

Run every command from the repository root with JDK 17 or newer. Build first:

```
javac --release 17 -d out -sourcepath src src/restaurantrush/Main.java
```

The recorded runs in `runs/` belong to the stage they are checked out with. This folder holds the Stage 3 runs; the Stage 1 and Stage 2 runs are in the `stage-1` and `stage-2` tags (`git checkout stage-1`).

## Stage 3 (tag `stage-3`)

### Automated checks

```
javac --release 17 -d out -sourcepath src test/restaurantrush/GameTests.java
java -cp out restaurantrush.GameTests
```

Expected: `164 checks passed, 0 failed`. The checks cover:

- **Stage 1 and 2 rules that must still hold**: order status rules, the Regular's loss of 8, the turn guards, tables, payment, abandonment, loss per waiting turn for Regular, VIP and Critic (8, 5, 12), the VIP's 10% discount, the locked bill, the Critic's food and late-service penalty, the six arrivals (including a VIP and a Critic), and that `GameController` contains no type checks. The food-rule checks that assume three dishes build that three-dish menu explicitly, because the Stage 3 menu has five entries;
- **ComboMeal**: Salad + Burger $21.60 and 2 units, Salad + Pasta $24.30 and 3 units, Burger + Pasta $29.70 and 3 units; a combo holds its two component items and is a `MenuItem`; a 3-unit combo is READY only after 3 units; the menu has five entries (the three dishes and two combos built from the menu's own dishes); Regular and VIP choose by `customerId mod 5`, the Critic takes the Salad + Pasta combo;
- **Happy Hour**: lasts exactly two turns, can be switched on once per game (a second activation is refused and does not restart it), VIP Burger $10.80, Regular Pasta $14.40, Critic's combo $19.44, no discount when off; an order placed before Happy Hour keeps its price, and an order taken during it keeps the discount after it ends; waiting customers recover 15 (capped at 100), served customers and customers who have left do not;
- **Host**: seats a queued customer, refuses an occupied table or a customer who is not waiting without changing anything, and the Waiter can still seat;
- **The recorded runs below**: results, Happy Hour offered only until used (and offered again after being declined), the Host seating while the Waiter takes the order in the same turn, rejected choices explained and followed by another prompt in the same turn.

### Reference values from the brief

| Case | Price | Prep units | Checked by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Combo: Salad + Burger | $21.60 | 2 | `GameTests` |
| Combo: Salad + Pasta | $24.30 | 3 | `GameTests` |
| Combo: Burger + Pasta | $29.70 | 3 | `GameTests` |
| VIP, Burger | $13.50 | 1 | `GameTests` |
| VIP, Burger, during Happy Hour | $10.80 | 1 | `GameTests` and the victory run (turn 1) |
| Regular, Pasta, during Happy Hour | $14.40 | 2 | `GameTests` |

java -cp out restaurantrush.Main --input runs/victory.txt

| 16 | | SEAT 6 | TAKE_ORDER 6 | COOK 6 | #6 arrives (it cannot finish paying in time) |
| 17 | | WAIT | SERVE 6 | WAIT | Cashier collects $9.00 from #5 (the fifth payment) |
| 18 | | WAIT | WAIT | WAIT | #6 becomes READY_TO_PAY too late to pay |

Choices that change the outcome (checked by replaying the run with a different choice):

- **Happy Hour.** Never switching it on gives $86.40 revenue and average satisfaction 80.2 instead of $83.70 and 81.2: Happy Hour costs $2.70 of revenue and buys one satisfaction point on average here, which matters when the average is close to 60.
- **Turn 13.** The run serves the Critic first (satisfaction 54 at payment). Taking #5's order first and serving the Critic in turn 14 costs the Critic a further 12, so it pays with 42 and the average falls from 81.2 to 78.8 (still a victory, with the same revenue).

### Reproducible defeat run

```
java -cp out restaurantrush.Main --input runs/defeat.txt
```

The manager switches Happy Hour on in turn 1, serves the VIP through to payment, and then only chooses WAIT. Expected ending: `DEFEAT`, 1 paid customer, revenue $10.80, average satisfaction 100.0, 1 served, 2 unhappy departures (customer 2, a Regular, leaves at the end of turn 16; customer 4, the Critic, at the end of turn 18), 18 turns used. A perfectly happy customer is not enough: all three targets must be met.

### Invalid input

`runs/victory.txt` contains a rejected Host choice (turn 1, `SEAT 5` before customer 5 exists) and a rejected Waiter choice (turn 6, `SERVE 3`). Each prints the reason and asks again in the same turn. `GameTests` checks the messages and that the matching prompt follows. A Happy Hour answer other than `y` or `n` is rejected and asked again (checked by an inline input in `GameTests`).

## Stage 2 (tag `stage-2`)

Check out the tag and use its `runs/` and tests.

- Automated checks: 108 pass.
- Victory run: `VICTORY`, 5 paid, $76.50, average 69.6, 5 served, 0 unhappy departures. The VIP pays $13.50, the Critic orders Pasta and, served 3 turns after ordering, pays with satisfaction 30.
- Defeat run: the VIP is served, then only WAIT. `DEFEAT`, 1 paid, $13.50, average 90.0, 2 unhappy departures.

## Stage 1 (tag `stage-1`)

- Defeat run: the manager only chooses WAIT. `DEFEAT`, 0 paid, $0.00, average N/A, 2 unhappy departures.
